CROSFIELD'S SPEECH. taken 33 voted for the amendment and 13 for the PROBABLE VISITS OF MR. LLOYD GEORGE AND :kilt. resolution. WINSTON CHURCHILL. Meeting of Teachers. The Annual Meeting of the Warrington Cam .ificated Class Teachers' Association was held in the Municipel Technical Institute on Saturday last. Mr. I. Bowen presided ovgr a large attendance of members. The report of the eecretary, and the belance sheet having been adopted, the chair was taken by Hr. F. N. Longshaw, President for the coming year. In a most interesting address Ur. Lengeh.aw dealt with, various matter l (:) immediate concern to class tlachers. Whileit recognising the improved conditions for ali grades of teachers since the Educat:on Act came into force, the speaker had reason to think that a wrong impression of the status of the class teachers was prevalent in many minds,and it was necessary that this impression should be removed. He also called attention to the large barrack schools existent, and the consequent lessenieg of the chances for promotion of class teachers to the position of head teachers. The following were elected to offiel for the ensuing yeer z—Vice-Presiderit, Mr. F. J. Emson ; treasurer, Mr. T. Edden ; secrete-yi Mr. S. W. Jeffery; committee, Misses E. Brookes, B. Mcl-eel, E. Pickavance, Messrs. Armstrong, Devon, Williams. Messrs. Edden and Jeffrey gave a report of +he pie - ceedings of the class teachers' corference recently held at Liverpool. Votes of thanks were accorded to Mr. Bowen for past services and to Mr. Longshaev for his address.

THE PROPOSED STREET AT ' WILDERSPOOL. Local Action in the Chancery Court. In the Liverpool Chancery Court on Monday, before Vice-Chancellor Hall, Messrs. Greenall, Whitley and Co., brought an action against Messrs. Mainwaring and Pemberton, to compel them to carry out their obligation with regard to the filling up of their clay pits on the land adjoining Wilderspool Causeway. Plaintiffs claimed damages for the non-fulfilment of the agreement between „themselves and the defendants. Defendants counter-claimed that the plaintiffs had not carried out their obligations in that they had not, according to the agreement, constructed a4O foot cinder roadway over a portion of their land lying between Wilderspool Causeway and Knutsford- road. Defendants also claimed damages for loss occa- sioned by Messrs. Greenall not carrying out their part , lof the agreement. The plaintiffs were represented by I Mr. afaberly and Mr. Lawrence (instructed by Messrs. 'Robert Davies and Company, Warrington) and the de- I fendants by Mr. Courthope Wilson (instructed by• IMessrs. Spencer, Wallhead, and Son). Defendant's case was that they purchased the land ! from Messrs. Mainwaring and Pemberton and that an agreement was entered into whereby the defendants I retained the power to take clay from the land for al certain number of years. In that agreement it was stipulated that a portion of the excavations should be filled in by tke 25th August 1902, and the remaining portion by the Ist April 1903. Defendants had not carried out that portion of the agreement and Plaintiffs sought to compel them to do so. Through the defendants not having filled in the excavations ' plaintiffs submitted they had lost the agricultural rent of the land. Plaintiffs required the defendants to carry, out their obligations as they had been approached by the London and North-Western Railway Company, who desired to take over the land but who declined to do so while it was in its present state. Plaintiffs feared that the Railway Company might obtain other land unless they were able shortly to place the plot in dispute before them. With regard to the road- way, the plaintiffs contended that as the Warrington Corporation had declined to pass the plans it was use- less for them to proceed with the work, and further urged that the agreement contained a clause to say that in the event of the Corporation refusing to pass the road their obligation in that direction ceased. Defendants case was that being in negotiation with the plaintiffs they had every reason to believe they , would get an extension of time for the filling up of the , land. They were prepared to carry out their • part of the agreement and were at present engaged filling in the land, though they could not complete the work before June of next year. Defenjants were prepared to pay Messrs. Greenall the agricultural rent which they sail they had lost, and to continue paying it until the excavations were filled in. With regard to the counter-claim, defendants had some land on the other side of the railway and this had, they contended, deteriorated in value on account of Messrs. Greenall not having made their part of the stipulated , roadway,, His Honour gave judgment to the effect that the defendants consented to an order for a specific perform- ance of the covenant to fill up the pits provided that the time for so doing was extended to the 24th June next, defendants to pay the general costs of the action. On the counter-claim the Vice-Chancellor held that the defendants were entitled to have a 40ft. road made from the London and North-Western Railway to Wilderspool-road, 35ft. of it to be cindered throughout, and made an order accordingly. The plaintiffs were ordered to pay defendants' costs on the counter-claim. The inquiry will be held in Chambers before the Registrar. An inquiry will be held in Chambers into the damages alleged to have been sustained by both parties.
